PART 4 — POKÉMON TCG POCKET (TCGP) — SEPARATE RULESET
=======================================================================

  [LOCKED] Pocket is distinct from converted Classic. Do not spread Classic-only Energy discard, Prize or deck changes into Pocket.
  [CURRENT] v0.69.185 source Pocket battle profile: 20-card deck, 5 opening cards, 3 Bench slots, 3-point victory target, and Pocket-style Energy generation. Confirm further Pocket-specific limits from current code before any modification.
  [LOCKED] Pocket card legality is by real gameplay identity and actual owned print/art records; alternate artwork cannot evade copy limits or grant copies.
  [LOCKED] Energy uses the Pocket Energy Zone and the player's selected one-to-three types where permitted. Multi-type generation is random/seeded, with visible next Energy kept meaningful.
  [LOCKED] Pocket Confusion, condition checkup, win points, Pokémon-ex points, draw/search effects, Supporters, Trainers, Tools, Stadiums and Fossils follow the applicable Pocket implementation rather than casually inheriting tabletop behaviour.
  [LOCKED] Coins, retreat, attacks, Ability triggers, Bench damage, healing, exact target selection, weakness and Energy must execute using actual rules, not a simplified approximation.
  [LOCKED] Cards whose complex mechanics are not fully implemented are marked PARTIAL/UNSUPPORTED and excluded from legal battles. Do not silently downgrade them to generic damage and label them FULL.
  [LOCKED] The card-data pipeline must preserve canonical gameplay identity, print variants, ownership, provenance, source/errata and release eligibility. New sets require reviewed records, not unverified guesses.
  [LOCKED] Pocket deck editing needs filters, clear deck, legitimate evolution chains, cards visible on screen, a playable hand and functional manual/automatic Energy choices.
  [LOCKED] Pocket battle should be card-first, responsive and touch-friendly: tap cards for attacks/abilities, drag cards into play, show attached Energy, place cards on actual open Bench positions, animate readable CPU turns and keep the selected sleeve consistent.
  [LOCKED] Developers' test decks do not award cards, wins, currency or achievements. Unsupported mechanics must show a clear reason when excluded.
  [CHECK] Earlier notes describe various implementation gaps for currently PARTIAL Pocket cards; re-evaluate against the actual latest catalogue before claiming the current percentage of FULL cards.

=======================================================================
PART 5 — ALL DECK BUILDERS / DECK LEGALITY / METADATA
=======================================================================

5.1 Required deck-building behaviour
  [LOCKED] Separate Classic and Pocket rules, card pools, copy limits, owned cards, Energy engines and legal deck sizes.
  [LOCKED] Builder output must be legal under the selected format, not merely 30/20 arbitrary card IDs.
  [LOCKED] Provide and preserve manual deck editing, automatic build/repair, save/rename/duplicate/delete/clear deck, visible selected deck, filters and card inspection.
  [LOCKED] Set restrictions, type filters (including dual types), rarity filters, owned filters, gameplay/strategy tags and select all/none are meaningful. Do not silently drop or reset filter state.
  [LOCKED] Normal builder respects owned quantities. Test Lab deliberately ignores ownership without changing actual collection or normal saved decks.
  [LOCKED] Player and CPU deck builders must use the same legality checks and correct set/card eligibility; opponent selection must accept every supported added expansion, including Gen 3 sets.
  [LOCKED] A 'random' deck should actually vary based on its chosen seed/options, and not repeat a fixed rotation of a few prebuilt lists. Preserve reasonable archetype quality while increasing variation.
  [LOCKED] A focused build around a named Pokémon / card must genuinely revolve around that card's win condition and line. Do not bolt on unrelated multi-stage lines just because they share a type.
  [LOCKED] Dual-type decks must account for real Energy needs and synergy, not only type labels.

5.2 Evolution / Energy / strategic coherence
  [LOCKED] Evolution chains must be complete enough to function, respecting allowed copies and current type/set restrictions. No orphan Stage 2s and no nonsensical multiple partially supported lines.
  [LOCKED] C-DECK-EVO-002 (11 Nov 2026): An AUTOMATICALLY CHOSEN Pokémon line must progress to its highest legal, owned, battle-ready evolution available in the selected card pool. If a Stage 1 such as Quilava is chosen as the attacker, it must also include a compatible Stage 2 (Typhlosion) when the species evolves further; do not mistake a PARTIAL/unowned/excluded Stage 2 for an unavailable species evolution and call Quilava a complete finished core. If no legal higher stage can be included, choose a different automatic core. An expressly user-focused standalone Basic in a restricted historical set remains allowed; 'Basic-only' deck filters intentionally limit evolution. Never replace the player's manually saved deck without permission.
  [LOCKED] C-DECK-FILL-003 (11 Nov 2026): A Fire/Water/etc. focus does not justify adding unrelated Pokémon with Colorless-only attack costs (e.g. Grass Scyther in an EX10 Fire deck). Prefer self-contained, type-coherent evolution partners and real standalone Basics. Do not use an unaccompanied evolvable Basic as arbitrary filler.
  [LOCKED] C-DECK-DUAL-004 (11 Nov 2026): Detect conditional type-changing Poké-Bodies (including Dual Armor) using their real activation condition. When an auto-builder deliberately selects such Pokémon, supply the trigger Basic Energy type, while retaining types needed for printed attack costs and respecting the two-type automatic deck plan. EX10 Magcargo: Fire+Fighting; EX10 Slowbro: Water+Psychic; Rocket's Scizor ex: Metal trigger (and Rocket's Scyther ex: Grass trigger). Never call a conditional secondary type active merely because the printed effect mentions it. Explicitly incompatible locked Energy-type filters should prevent choosing that as an auto core rather than inventing a third type.
  [LOCKED] C-DECK-CPU-005 (11 Nov 2026): The opponent generator must USE the full legal Energy profile returned by the shared Classic deck builder. Never replace a purposeful Fire/Fighting, Water/Psychic or other two-type evolution/ability plan with only the requested category (such as Fire). The CPU must be capable of activating the same abilities and paying the same costs as the player.
  [LOCKED] C-DECK-MANUAL-006 (11 Nov 2026): A conditional Dual Armor activation is an auto-builder *recommendation*, not a mandatory Energy cost. A manually configured deck without the optional trigger may remain legal when all printed attack costs can be paid. Its automatic Energy recommendation should include the trigger when doing so fits without dropping required attack types.
  [LOCKED] For two-stage evolution decks, consider Rare Candy or equivalent legal accelerator when the relevant set/format supports it.
  [LOCKED] Hand attachments, Zone Energy, Special Energy, attack costs and energy acceleration must all be modelled accurately in deck construction.
  [LOCKED] Don't select Stadiums, Energy Tools, healing or retrieval cards whose conditions cannot be met by the deck. Example: a Stadium boosting an Energy type absent from the deck is not strategic synergy.
  [LOCKED] Synergy tags are causal, not decorative. A sleep-lock deck should combine actual Sleep application with payoffs, condition retention, healing/stall, stadiums and consistency. A high-HP stall deck should favor healing and retreat reduction. An Energy-ramp deck should support an attacker that benefits from extra Energy.
  [LOCKED] Consider deck roles: main attacker, backup, evolution support, search, draw, Energy acceleration, Energy recovery, healing, status control, disruption, switching/retreat, Stadium/Tool synergy, counterplay and board consistency.
  [LOCKED] Do not include bad 'filler' simply to hit the deck length. Provide candidate quality and legality diagnostics rather than quietly assembling an ineffective deck.
  [LOCKED] Avoid CPU autobuild loops that appear frozen for minutes; permit progress, cancellation or bounded work so the application stays responsive.
  [LOCKED] Keep user-selected focus cards/strategy, opponent set restrictions and existing saved deck preferences when repairing a deck.

5.3 Card metadata standards
  [LOCKED] Every card entry needs correct identity (set + number), printed name, evolution stage/line, types, HP, weakness/resistance, retreat, rarity/ownership mapping, attack cost/energy, damage, printed text and every attack/Power/Body/Trainer/Special Energy rule.
  [LOCKED] Preserve alternative print art and ownership separately from gameplay identity. Prevent ID collisions, duplication and wrong imagery.
  [LOCKED] Role/status tags must be earned by actual gameplay effects. Status immunity does not equal inflicting a status. Self-sleep does not make a Sleep-control deck. Defensive prevention does not count as direct attack damage.
  [LOCKED] Metadata used by AI/deck building must distinguish healing, protection, Sleep/Poison/Burn/Paralysis/Confusion, Energy ramp, Energy discard/recovery, search/draw, spread/snipe, retreat reduction, disruption, scaling damage and other relevant archetypes.
  [LOCKED] Multi-condition choice effects should expose all permitted conditions, not a default hardcoded one.
  [LOCKED] If a card is supported only with an explicit converted interpretation, record that conversion in its set documentation and tests.
  [LOCKED] All card effect data and metadata must remain consistent with actual engine behavior; don't tag a card as viable if its mechanic is unsupported or incorrect.

=======================================================================
PART 6 — AI OPPONENT AND BATTLE EXPERIENCE
=======================================================================

  [LOCKED] CPU obeys the same rules as the player: timing, Energy Zone order, retreat, evolution timing, deck copy limits, discard costs, once-per-turn actions, targeting and Trainer restrictions.
  [LOCKED] AI should actively evaluate legal choices instead of random spam. It's acceptable to randomize among approximately equal choices, not among obviously harmful and useful actions without reason.
  [LOCKED] AI should prioritize KOs, effective targets, sensible Energy attachment, evolution, healing damaged Pokémon, retreating vulnerable Actives, status synergies and efficient Trainers.
  [LOCKED] CPU should not replace a beneficial Stadium with a harmful/irrelevant one simply because it can. Consider existing Stadium and marginal value to both sides.
  [LOCKED] CPU should not repeatedly use effects with no legal targets (e.g., Super Energy Retrieval with no recoverable Energy).
  [LOCKED] CPU must pay actual Energy discard requirements. Don't allow it to keep charged Energy while claiming to use a discard-cost attack, or permanently refuse a playable move due to a mistaken cost check.
  [LOCKED] Difficulty tiers (Beginner/Intermediate/Advanced/Expert where implemented) should reflect real decision quality, not rule-breaking or invented resources.
  [LOCKED] Opponent deck building must finish in bounded time, observe set/archetype/difficulty filters, and generate actual playable legal decks.
  [LOCKED] Show CPU actions, coin flips, Trainer use, Energy movements and damage in readable central presentation at human-visible pacing. Don't instantly complete an entire hidden turn and leave the player guessing.
  [LOCKED] The CPU should make choices for defending side effects when printed ('your opponent chooses'), rather than incorrectly allowing the acting attacker to choose.

=======================================================================
PART 7 — TEST LAB / DEVELOPER TOOLS
=======================================================================

  [LOCKED] Test Lab is a separate sandbox for both Classic and Pocket. Its purpose is fast effect/combo testing without collection grinding.
  [LOCKED] Test Lab can select any existing supported card regardless of normal ownership; normal battles remain ownership-restricted.
  [LOCKED] Test Lab allows placing real cards on either side's Active/Bench, into hand/deck/discard/Prize zones as appropriate, and staging damage, Energy, status and turn context.
  [LOCKED] Inert Substitute cards/targets can be used to test effects and damage without adding new collectible cards, Powers or attacks of their own.
  [LOCKED] Test Lab must use the REAL combat engine and real card selection prompts. It is not a fake scripted animation mode.
  [LOCKED] Allow clear direct selection of Active/Bench and access to deck/hand/discard card pickers. Keep the UI usable on ONE mobile screen where possible, not a sprawling desktop panel.
  [LOCKED] Test Lab decks have their OWN saved Pocket and Classic libraries, distinct from normal saved decks and actual collection. Save, rename, delete and reopen them independently.
  [CURRENT] v0.69.185 lineage includes persistent Test Lab libraries and whitelisted CPU filter preferences (schema 2).
  [LOCKED] Test Lab card filters and CPU-opponent filters are separate and must remain visible and persistent where designed. Don't fix one by accidentally removing the other.
  [LOCKED] Test Lab matches grant no coins, packs, new card ownership, achievements, regular battle wins or progression records. Do not save fake damage/board state as a real player's official result.
  [LOCKED] Normal and Test Lab game modes should not mutate each other's deck selections, collection records, filters or battle statistics.

=======================================================================
PART 8 — CARDS / EXPANSIONS: CONTENT COMPLETENESS STANDARD
=======================================================================

  [LOCKED] Add expansions in the agreed chronological/selected order; current sequence continues EX Deoxys (ex8), EX Emerald (ex9), EX Unseen Forces (ex10), EX Delta Species (ex11), EX Legend Maker (ex12), EX Holon Phantoms (ex13), EX Crystal Guardians (ex14). Future sets follow release order unless user changes direction.
  [LOCKED] New expansion work covers ALL actual cards, not just the headline Pokémon. Include Trainers, Supporters, Stadiums, Tools, Special Energy, evolution chains and Pokémon with non-obvious effects.
  [LOCKED] Use exact set IDs and card numbers with authoritative card text and errata. Do not invent unreadable text or silently create Pokémon that were not in the released set.
  [LOCKED] Inspect interactions with older cards: Rocket, Gym, Neo, Delta, EX, Special Energy, existing custom Snap sets, status effects, Stadiums and Tools. Card references to 'Dark Pokémon', 'Rocket's', 'Trainer', 'Pokémon-ex', etc. must recognise compatible older and newer cards when the printed wording intends them to.
  [LOCKED] Expand the player builder, CPU deck selector, opponent filters, Test Lab and catalogue together. Missing an expansion from opponent selection is an incomplete release.
  [LOCKED] Every new attack/Power/Body must have an accurate description and a working effect, or an explicit gated/unsupported status. Do not publish 'fully supported' based only on cards loading or two random attacks finishing.
  [LOCKED] Move metadata and synergy tags are required in the same expansion pass, not a future afterthought.
  [LOCKED] Card search, movement, prizes, discard, choice and Energy must be validated with both success and failure/edge-case scenarios.
  [LOCKED] Work can be delivered in staged batches if the user wants, but the whole-set audit and tests must be performed before claiming completion.

Release checklist for EVERY new expansion:
  [ ] Identify exact set, date, collector numbers, card roster, rarity and identifiers.
  [ ] Load and map all cards and artwork correctly, retaining existing collection/ownership data.
  [ ] Implement every attack/Power/Body/Trainer/Stadium/Tool/Special Energy with printed-effect fidelity.
  [ ] Record LuckyDex-specific conversions/unsupported aspects explicitly.
  [ ] Verify Energy Zone, physical Basic/Special Energy, hand, discard, deck and prize interactions.
  [ ] Verify conditions, prevention, status, Weakness, HP/damage and Energy cost calculations.
  [ ] Verify target selection, optional outcomes and choices on both sides.
  [ ] Test play vs same expansion, selected earlier expansions and mixed decks.
  [ ] Player manual builder, autofill/strategy builder, rarity/set filters, CPU decks and Test Lab all recognise the set.
  [ ] AI chooses meaningful actions, pays costs, uses healing and can complete matches.
  [ ] Test normal win/lose/draw/exhaustion, saved deck reload and basic Android portrait UI.
  [ ] Record exact test counts, test failures and testing limitations.
  [ ] Update this rules file, per-set documentation and change history if any rule/exception was added.
  [ ] Bump version consistently, package changed files only, give correct Termux instructions.

=======================================================================
PART 9 — TESTING / REGRESSION / RELEASE QUALITY
=======================================================================

9.1 Mandatory test matrix
  [LOCKED] A rule bug requires an outcome-level regression: verify the card, zone, counter, target and final state. 'Effect function ran' is NOT a sufficient assertion.
  [LOCKED] Test with both sides where it matters: player and CPU use the same effects; opponent-chooses and owner-chooses are different.
  [LOCKED] Test positive, negative and boundary cases: 0/1/2 eligible cards, heads/tails, no legal target, empty deck, full Bench, two identically named Pokémon, expired lock, no Energy, mixed Basic/Special Energy, prize card unavailable and status already present.
  [LOCKED] Test normal play and Test Lab. Sandbox-only success is not proof that in-match resource flows work.
  [LOCKED] After shared-engine changes, run relevant old expansion suites and the broader historical suite where feasible. Describe stale test assertions separately from actual gameplay failures.
  [LOCKED] Never alter a failing test only to force a pass. If a rule changed with user approval, update its expectation and explain the rule change; preserve a regression for the new intended result.
  [LOCKED] Check card metadata and decks as well as battle resolution: legality, ownership limits, set/rarity filters, evolution lines, 30/20 card size, energy synergy and AI archetypes.
  [LOCKED] Check interface decisions: clear labels, one visible legal target, tapped-board selection, correct slot identity, displayed Energy, no overflow/overlaps, coin positions, damage preview and opponent action readability.
  [LOCKED] Check save migration/backward compatibility, installed app ID/signer and all version labels.
  [LOCKED] If a check cannot be performed (e.g., no Android device), say so and provide a small user-focused manual test scenario.

9.2 Permanent named regression scenarios
  R01 Energy Charge: Basic and Special Energy from separate discard zones -> chosen cards shuffle into DRAW DECK -> deck count increases -> Basic Energy can later be drawn -> normal attachment cap still applies.
  R02 Manectric ex Disconnect: opponent cannot use non-Supporter Trainers on affected next turn; may use allowed Supporter; lock expires on time; clear error text.
  R03 Magcargo Knock Over: if Stadium relevant, choices display 'Keep Stadium' / 'Discard Stadium', same printed damage, correct outcome after selection.
  R04 Salamence ex Flame Jet: selecting target happens by tapping the correct opposing board Pokémon, including when two same-name cards share the Bench.
  R05 Energy Flow: returned Basic Energy appears in the HAND as playable hand cards, no silent loss.
  R06 Moltres/Metagross/Camerupt recovery: eligible Energy chosen from correct discard zones and attached to chosen targets, preserving costs and side effects.
  R07 Trainer restrictions: playing a Supporter does not on its own block ordinary Item Trainers; legitimate effect-specific locks are visible.
  R08 Scyther: an attack-specific next-turn restriction does not disable other legal attacks.
  R09 Special Energy: Darkness/Metal/Rainbow and set-specific Energy really provide printed quantities/effects; no fake multiple-unit Rainbow payments.
  R10 Pokémon-ex KOs and prize exceptions: correct multiple-Prize awards and empty-shell/no-Prize effects.
  R11 Bench: play/target/promote/evolve from correct slot, correct choice owner and no invalid automatic leftmost target.
  R12 CPU: stadium use, energy-discard costs, heal only when useful, no effect-without-target spam.
  R13 Autobuilder: random variation, complete evolution lines, genuine strategy focus, no irrelevant Energy Stadiums, 30/20 cards and copy/ownership legality.
  R14 Test Lab: owned restriction bypass only in sandbox, independent saved decks/filters, no real rewards or stats.
  R15 Full UI: readable mobile cards, landscape Stadium proportion, battle mat size, direct targeting, clear optional outcome labels.
  R16 Card support: no 'special effect' placeholder counted as complete; each included attack/card has actually executed the relevant outcome.
  R17 [C-ENERGY-001..012] Fresh Basic deck search -> eligible ordered Energy Zone; effect-based ramp can add extra attached Basic; search shows player type choice and never invents absent Energy.
  R18 [C-ENERGY-004/007] Neo Energy Charge/Time Capsule/Riptide, Expedition Power Charge/Beating Wings, Neo Pilfer, Skyridge Energy Recycle, Aquapolis Town Volunteers always put eligible discarded/attached Basic into actual DRAW DECK.
  R19 [C-ENERGY-005/006] Base Blastoise Rain Dance requires Water Energy physically in hand. Normal hand Basic/Special uses ONE quota independently of normal Zone quota; effect-based ramp is extra.
  R20 [C-ENERGY-003/008] Normal supply order stays visible. Fresh typed search may consume first matching type; top-N physical deck checks are never converted to free searches.
  R21 [C-ENERGY-004] Aquapolis Power Plant trades an actual Basic hand Energy for selected eligible real discarded Energy, including separate Basic ledger; no fake or self-refilling Zone recovery.
  R22 [C-ENERGY-013/014] Neo/e-Card Darkness bonus after Weakness vs EX-era before Weakness only on eligible attackers; Double Rainbow -10 on Swift and Bench.
  R23 [C-CHOICE-001] EX Emerald Lava Burn base+Bench, PokéNav top-three choice and actual reorder even when zero eligible; Regice ex selected Basic/Special Energy discard.
  R24 [C-METADATA-001] Metadata for real Energy recovery vs damage scaling, Lady Outing legal autobuilder scoring, CPU/player 30-card deck legality.
  R25 [C-EX16-ENERGY-001] Power Keepers Darkness/Metal/Multi reprint damage/defense/conditional provision matches equivalent EX-era printed Energy (against other Pokémon types and multi-Energy attachments); zero extra effect from the wrong type.
  R26 [C-EX16-CHOICE-002] A printed choice of opponent Pokémon and individual attached Energy is made by the PLAYER NAMED ON THE CARD, with actual Basic/Special choice and correct discard zone. When the defending player is told to choose which hand cards to discard (Mawile Pull Away), the defender chooses each card rather than suffering a random discard.


9.3 Honest release notes
  Every patch note should state: baseline version, target version, changed files, exact bugs/decisions, tests actually run (and failures), known untested areas, required previous version, extraction target, Git/Termux commands, and one or two decisive in-app verification scenarios.
  Never extrapolate 'all cards correct' from an automated attack smoke sweep. Combinatorial card interactions require targeted examples and cross-era tests.
  Never promise that Android touch has been tested if only Node/browser tests were available.

=======================================================================
PART 10 — MANDATORY LIVING-DOCUMENT UPDATE PROTOCOL
=======================================================================

THIS PART APPLIES TO EVERY FUTURE CHAT, EXPANSION, FIX AND AGREED RULE CHANGE.

10.1 At the START of each new LuckyDex development chat
  1. Read this entire file from the latest ZIP/repository before editing anything.
  2. Identify the exact app/source version and the patch ZIP extraction convention.
  3. Read recent set-release notes and relevant existing tests.
  4. Check the current request against [LOCKED] rules and [CHECK] conflicts.
  5. Do not depend solely on conversational memory; code, tests and this document must agree.
  6. If the user reports a bug that contradicts a passing test, first investigate whether the test asserts the WRONG destination, wrong player, wrong version or a missing UI interaction.

10.2 When the user approves or specifies ANY new rule
  1. Quote/restate the rule in implementation terms: who, when, zone, limits, options, visible UI, and exceptions.
  2. Assign it a permanent rule ID (for example C-ENERGY-006, C-CHOICE-012, P-ABILITY-004, AI-STRATEGY-008, GAME-ARCADE-003).
  3. Add/change the appropriate [LOCKED] clause in THIS FILE before final delivery.
  4. Mark and resolve any superseded rule instead of leaving contradictory instructions.
  5. Add at least one regression test tied to the rule ID. Add failure and edge cases where relevant.
  6. Update any set-specific documentation and player-facing explanation made obsolete by the rule.
  7. Implement the game change and verify it against older sets and both sides of play.
  8. Bump THIS DOCUMENT version/date and include its updated copy with the patch.
  9. Describe the new/changed rule and its test in the patch release note.
 10. If implementation is intentionally deferred, label the rule [LOCKED — NOT YET IMPLEMENTED], identify the blocker, and do not claim it works.

10.3 When adding a new expansion
  1. Add a dated entry under the SET REGISTER (Part 11): set code, count, status, baseline/release version, coverage tested and any conversion exceptions.
  2. Apply the Part 8 expansion checklist to EVERY card family, Trainer, Stadium, Tool and Special Energy.
  3. Add new effect patterns to a shared rule heading when the mechanic is reusable, not hidden solely in a one-off card handler.
  4. If the expansion reveals an interaction with older cards, document and test the cross-set interaction with exact card identifiers.
  5. If the new mechanic contradicts a previously agreed LuckyDex format rule, do not silently reinterpret it. Record [CHECK], propose a faithful adaptation and get user agreement.
  6. Update per-set notes, the master test matrix and this file in the same patch, even if the rules are unchanged (record 'no global rule change').

10.4 When fixing a bug
  1. Record observed wrong behaviour, correct expected behaviour, original cause and affected versions.
  2. Determine if existing rules/tests were wrong (Energy Charge .183 is the model example).
  3. Add a regression that would have failed BEFORE the fix and passes AFTER.
  4. Update this file if a rule interpretation changed, a new exception was discovered or a previous rule was expressed ambiguously.
  5. Avoid writing 'bug is a feature' merely because the current code was designed that way.
  6. Check other cards sharing the same effect path and previous expansions using that API.

10.5 When rules and implementation disagree
  A. Preserve evidence of both claims and tag the issue [CHECK].
  B. State exactly what the running build currently does versus what was agreed.
  C. Do not silently change balance/rules or falsify tests.
  D. Resolve based on the latest user decision and printed card accuracy.
  E. Once resolved, update the actual rule, tests, UI explanation, set notes, version and history.

10.6 Required CHANGELOG entry template (append; don't erase history)
  Date: YYYY-MM-DD
  Document revision: x.y -> x.z
  App version / source baseline: v...
  Rule IDs changed: ...
  Status: [LOCKED]/[EXCEPTION]/[CHECK]/[BUG]
  User decision / verified card wording: ...
  Previous rule/implementation: ...
  New requirement / exception: ...
  Code locations: ...
  New/updated tests: ...
  Cross-set regression coverage: ...
  Android device QA: tested / not tested
  Known caveats / follow-up: ...

10.7 Single-file upkeep
  Keep this as one easy-to-read plain TXT file because the user uses new chats and phone uploads. Extra technical/per-set docs may exist, but they MUST link or point back to this file and must not quietly supersede its shared rules.
  Include this file in EVERY future rules-changing changed-files patch (for example under docs/LUCKYDEX_MASTER_RULES.txt).
  Do not increment the document revision just because a build number changes with no content change; DO append the expansion register/status note or 'no global rule changes' where needed.
  If requested, a short project-level prompt can point all future conversations to this file, but the full detail lives here rather than being limited by custom-instruction space.


10.8 [LOCKED] C-RELEASE-GATE-001 — SOURCE-TRUTH PREFLIGHT (NO GUESSES)
  At expansion start, identify and verify: exact ZIP/source commit and version; scripts/registries/picker/opponents; exact English card-print set ID, numbered count and secret/alternate prints; authority URL for EVERY printed card; pre-existing mechanics; cross-era conversion rules; intended release order and all required running assets. Read THIS FILE and the most recent postrelease audit before coding. Do not extrapolate printed rules from names or other expansions. Record baseline passing/failing tests before a change so old failures are not attributed to new work.
  Compare printed set record count against catalogue with exact number/ID continuity, names, type, stage, HP, evolved-from, Weakness and Resistance MAGNITUDE, retreat, rarity, image, attacks, costs, damage, ability name/type/trigger, Trainer/Stadium/Special Energy text and Basic Energy identity. Do NOT infer effect correctness from a card scan or an artwork URL.

10.9 [LOCKED] C-RELEASE-GATE-002 — EXPLICIT PER-CARD EXECUTION CONTRACT
  For EVERY attack, Power, Body, Trainer, Stadium, Tool or Energy effect write down:
    (a) acting/choosing side, victim side, and who may DECLINE; (b) exact trigger and
    play/evolution/attack/KO/retreat/between-turn window; (c) prior conditions and
    Energy costs, plus any additional discard, reveal or self-KO costs; (d) source,
    destination and ownership of each card/Energy/counter, Basic Zone vs physical
    hand/deck/discard, including special Energy; (e) choice order, number and
    eligible targets/hand positions; (f) hidden vs visible information; (g) timing
    of damage, Weakness, Resistance, prevention, healing, statuses and KO/Prizes;
    (h) exact duration and cleanup; (i) CPU strategy and touchscreen presentation.
  Trace real BEFORE and AFTER game state in tests. A registered handler, a no-op
  fallback, or merely opening a picker NEVER counts as a verified effect.
  Every optional 'may' starts with a real Use/Decline decision (player and CPU),
  and Decline must leave all source/destination zones unchanged. A forced action
  with a legal target must never quietly skip. Choice ownership MUST agree with
  printed text; card identity cannot be shown for facedown opponent hand picks.

10.10 [LOCKED] C-RELEASE-GATE-003 — INTERACTION DEPENDENCY MATRIX
  BEFORE enabling a card map both forward and reverse synergies across:
    Stadium <-> Power/Body/attack/status; Tool <-> attack/evolution/ability;
    Special Energy <-> costs/activation/KO/discard/retrieval; Supporter/Trainer
    <-> hand/deck/Zone/discard; Bench/Active/KO/retreat <-> triggered abilities;
    evolution stages <-> support Powers; card classifications (ex, Star, LV.X,
    delta, Dark, Rocket, Fossil) <-> limitations and printed interactions.
  Test an interaction WITH its required partner, WITHOUT the partner, and WITH
  the partner disabled/suppressed or out of range. Test opponent and player
  separately and verify the CPU makes a sensible choice. Example historical
  failures: Cacturne only works with Sidney's Stadium; Altaria heals under
  Drake's Stadium; Xatu must pair with a COMPLETE Stage 2 ex beneficiary;
  Power-disabled cards cannot silently trigger; Tool-demanding attacks need
  a legal Tool. Capture new global interactions in this file, not merely notes.

10.11 [LOCKED] C-RELEASE-GATE-004 — DECKBUILDER & CPU INVARIANTS
  For BOTH owned player decks and all-cards Test Lab/CPU, test:
  * Exactly 30 real cards, legal per-name copy cap, owned copies when relevant,
    legal species/form limits, allowed set/rarity/Stage restriction and one
    playable Basic; never pull a gated/unsupported card.
  * All selected attacking Pokemon can actually pay at least one useful attack
    from the computed Basic Energy Zone and permitted physical Special Energy;
    do not add off-type lines just because synergy metadata says 'preferred'.
  * If the user explicitly chooses ONE Energy type, only that type is planned;
    Colorless costs are payable, but no unrequested colored Energy type may
    leak in. Widen automatically ONLY if type selection was not locked and
    explain this in the returned plan.
  * Complete Basic->Stage 1->Stage 2 evolution chain where feasible; NEVER add
    a stranded Stage 1, a useless Pokémon-ex-only support or an out-of-set
    evolution. Baby ancestry and LV.X level-up are distinct from Stage 1/2;
    LV.X must retain its previous-level attacks and be legal only as printed.
  * Include real consumable Tools/Energy/Stadium/Bench partners for conditional
    cards. Support effects must change composition, not just appear as tags.
  * Focused build on every battle-enabled Pokémon with a four-copy synthetic
    collection: PASS, or provide a machine-testable explanation for impossible
    set/type/ownership constraints. Do not silently 'pass' an unbuilt focus.
  * Seeded CPU builds across ALL eight Basic Energy types, all nine strategy
    archetypes, single-set / selected mixed-set pools, with no strategy locks,
    blank actions, hidden-hand peeking, endless choice loops or habitual skips
    for beneficial effects. Explicitly report unsupported types while staged.

10.12 [LOCKED] C-RELEASE-GATE-005 — TESTS MUST DEMONSTRATE OUTCOMES
  Minimum release matrix for EACH new effect family:
    successful and ineligible use, no-target case, zero/multiple target case,
    mandatory and declined optional paths, player and CPU as actor, appropriate
    self/opponent ownership, attack/Bench/KO/checkpoint timing, stacked effects
    and ability suppression. Count real affected cards/counters, not only logs.
  For random effects fix RNG seeds and assert BOTH heads/tails branches.
  For search/discard/transfers assert the exact CARD ID and ZONE before/after;
  do not let card-count coincidences mask a wrong destination. For attacks,
  compare printed damage at base, Weakness/Resistance, protected Bench,
  multipliers/additive damage and copied attacks including non-Energy costs.
  Repeat an EX-era regression selection after cross-era shared-engine edits.
  Record any known baseline failures separately and never relabel them 'fixed'.
  Attack smoke tests are necessary but INSUFFICIENT: a no-crash attack can be
  entirely wrong; each unique effect family also requires outcome tests.

10.13 [LOCKED] C-RELEASE-GATE-006 — NON-NEGOTIABLE CARD LEDGER & RELEASE WORDING
  Keep one ledger row for EACH print with: ID/name, printed metadata verified
  (Y/N), attack handler tested (Y/N/N/A), activated/triggered ability tested,
  relevant dependency tested, owner/CPU choice tested, deckbuild tested, result
  FULL/PARTIAL/BLOCKED and the remaining defect. Source registration is NOT
  battle readiness, and battle readiness is NOT proof of print-level fidelity.
  Stage releases may list all cards in the collection but enable only cards
  with complete executable effects and corresponding tests. A required mechanic
  absent from the current engine remains BLOCKED with an explicit issue.
  NEVER say a set is 'complete' until ALL printed cards are battle-enabled,
  print-vs-runtime effects are individually audited, legal focused and CPU
  builds are verified or explained, and targeted plus cross-set tests pass.
  Even 'complete' means SOURCE completion only unless real Android touch QA,
  artwork and CPU matches were actually performed. Separate test classes,
  passing counts, skipped assertions, known limitations and user-visible steps.

10.14 [LOCKED] C-RELEASE-GATE-007 — HANDOFF AND UI
  Preserve prior versions, apply source-relative incremental patches and include
  this MASTER RULES file in every rules-changing ZIP. Increment app/versionCode,
  produce actual reproducible tests, verify ZIP paths and list required baseline.
  Prefer player card ART with no duplicate descriptive overlay where artwork
  loads; on failure show readable text. Facing-down card position choices must
  not reveal hidden card identities. The Android UI, CPU and Test Lab must use
  the SAME validated action/choice state machine, not separate placeholder logic.
  In release notes include: exact completed/gated counts, code changes, tested
  interaction matrix and matchups, full command/output summary, known gaps,
  install block, no false phone-test claim, next gated subset by card ID.

10.14A [LOCKED] C-RELEASE-GATE-008 — SMALL-BATCH OUTCOME AND EVOLUTION CERTIFICATION (v2.16)
  * Do not count a Stage 1 card as set-only focus-ready when its evolution is
    required by the complete-family policy but not yet enabled. An attack-smoke
    pass is NOT a focus-deck pass: independently test each stage in the allowed
    set/type pool with four owned copies and test the exact reason for failure.
  * For split evolutionary trees (Wurmple->Silcoon->Beautifly and
    Wurmple->Cascoon->Dustox) validate each COMPLETE playable branch with all
    required earlier stages, and never combine incompatible branches as one line.
  * Write assertions against ATTACK OUTPUT after weakness/resistance and target
    protection. A printed Bench 10 must not be mistaken for Active 10 when the
    Active is Lightning-weak (+10), and a defeated Active may be removed before
    reading its final damage: inspect KO log/discard rather than dereferencing it.
  * When an attack says '10 for each damage counter', remember battle-state
    damage is stored as HP units (damage 30 equals 3 counters, so bonus 30), not
    as a counter tally. Test injured AND uninjured attacker/defender cases.
  * Test protection on the next actual opponent attack, including a threshold
    hit and an above-threshold hit, AND with Weakness applied; creating an
    attackGuard or modifier object alone is not an outcome test.
  * On-card conditional healing such as Giga Drain/Leech Seed must use the
    damage actually dealt, not just printed attack damage; test nullified hits.
  * New attack target choices must be confirmed in the actual effect-choice
    state, including a Bench-selected path. Coin and status timing must be
    asserted in the right between-turn phase, never guessed from final status.
  * Reuse verified shared effects but record if a selected pool cannot produce
    a complete line; never un-gate a Pokémon just to satisfy a focus builder.

10.14B [LOCKED] C-RELEASE-GATE-009 — EFFECT DISPATCH AND CONDITIONAL SMOKE INPUTS (v2.17)
  * All new printed-effect rules must be registered in the correct dispatch
    chain and dispatch EXACT rule IDs before a broad set-prefix handler.
    Adding a generic dp1:* handler may silently mask previously working
    dp1:72:splashAbout or other older cases. Always rerun both preceding
    batches' specific outcome tests, not just the newest batch.
  * A 'can legally attack' smoke test must construct the *printed legal*
    target state first: Prinplup Brine requires an already damaged opposing
    Pokemon. Similarly, searches need eligible cards and a free Bench space;
    conditional attacks may require a status, Stadium, hand card or Energy.
    A missing prerequisite in a smoke test is not evidence of a card bug.
  * For attacks with an optional number of Bench targets, test selecting zero,
    one and multiple distinct eligible Pokemon. 'Choose up to two' must show
    an explicit decline path, never require two available targets, and still
    apply unrelated next-turn locks if targets are declined.
  * For deck search/attachment (Grotle Synthesis), separately test a chosen
    PHYSICAL Basic Energy drawn from the deck and the ordered Energy Zone:
    exactly one Energy arrives on the chosen target, the actual source loses
    one, and shuffle/visibility work as printed. CPU must not conjure a new
    Energy token or accept the wrong source type.
  * Damage-checkpoints, self-KO recoil, conditional statuses and next-turn
    restrictions require explicit log/state assertions. In particular, a
    self-knockout can remove the active slot, so examine the KO log, and an
    attack that does nothing against a currently awake defender must remain
    playable unless its printed text forbids announcing the attack.

10.14C [LOCKED] C-RELEASE-GATE-010 — FOCUSED ENERGY PLAN (v2.17)
  * A single-type focused deck must include a complete permitted evolution
    line AND at least one payable attack for every included stage. An older
    pre-evolution's OPTIONAL typed move is not an obligatory Energy type
    when it has a Colorless/otherwise payable attack. Example: Onix has
    Colorless Headbutt, so Steelix with Metal Energy alone is usable.
  * Never generalise this pruning to pre-DP sets without separate tests:
    older type-dependent partners, attack conditions and dual-type bonuses
    still require their own plan. Test selected-set filters for every line
    and give explicit errors if a higher evolution remains battle-gated.
  * CPU opponent-generation is a SEPARATE release gate from focused player
    decks. A focused Metal Steelix build does not prove the randomized
    DP-only Metal CPU generator can find a legal deck. Document both checks.

10.14D [LOCKED] C-RELEASE-GATE-011 — BABY EVOLUTION / EVOLUTION STACK DEPENDENCY (v2.18)
  * Never count a Baby Pokemon as an optional harmless tag when the evolved
    attack explicitly checks for that Baby beneath it. The focused auto
    builder must include the actual named Baby (Azurill/Marill/Azumarill,
    Budew/Roselia/Roserade) with a playable attack/evolution route.
    The relevant named Baby must exist and be battle-enabled in the chosen
    set. Do not introduce off-filter Energy to make a support Baby attack.
  * Baby Evolution is a once-per-turn, voluntary Poké-Power, not an
    ordinary Stage 1 play from hand: test offered/declined Power, source
    hand selection, correct matching evolution, preserved stack, healing,
    disabled-by-Condition, timing and CPU selection. Attack-level checks
    must independently verify both with-Baby and without-Baby outcomes.
  * All multi-target, Energy-count and between-turn damage examples must
    set up a non-weak target when the test is for raw damage and explicitly
    account for Poison/checkup ticks and Pokémon KOs.

10.14E [LOCKED] C-RELEASE-GATE-012 — TRAINER REPRINT OUTCOME AND ARTWORK (v2.18)
  * Matching an old rule ID is not enough to call a Trainer reprint done.
    Run actual hand -> play -> target -> destination -> end-state tests,
    including both coin outcomes and any opponent/owner choices.
  * When retrieving a Pokémon and attachments from a Super Scoop Up, the
    *whole* evolution stack, attached Special Energy, Tools and Basic Energy
    must return on heads; tails must leave that Pokémon untouched. For the
    DP1 reprint, synthetic recovered Basic Energy should use the matching
    DP1 printing rather than unrelated older-set artwork where identity is
    lost to the Energy Zone. Respect the application's hand limit.
  * Check selected source Pokémon, actual attached Basic Energy subtype,
    and different destination Pokémon for Energy Switch. The deckbuilder
    must preserve single-type restrictions even if a supporting Pokémon
    has another native type. The mono-Metal CPU generation gap from v0.69.225 was fixed in
    v0.69.226 by testing a payable attack at each included Pokemon; keep
    per-Energy CPU regressions to detect future recurrence.
  * In stage releases, report exact enabled/gated numbers and test results,
    and leave all nonimplemented LV.X and triggered Powers gated.

10.14F [LOCKED] C-RELEASE-GATE-013 — CHOICE PRIVACY, REAL TARGETS, REAL OUTCOMES (v2.19)
  * An effect that chooses an opposing hand card WITHOUT LOOKING must show
    facedown positions only, not card identities or a random chosen card.
    Test a known two-card hand, choose position 1, verify the exact physical
    card is moved to the correct discard/deck destination, and ensure
    the other card remains. Test the opponent/CPU side too.
  * Printed optional effects must offer both an actual decline and a legal
    accept target. Test both outcomes, not just that a popup or action
    exists. If a target requires attached Energy, an empty slot is excluded.
  * Printed BEFORE DAMAGE switching (e.g., DP Magneton Magnetic Ray) must
    change the active defender BEFORE base damage, Weakness, Resistance,
    damage prevention and KO evaluation. Test both choose and decline with
    distinct, Energy-qualified Bench targets; preserve defending side.
  * Trainer and Poké-Power coin outcomes must be tested independently using
    deterministic seeds; test the exact card/entity destination, the right
    source zone (hand, deck, ordinary discard, Basic-Energy ledger), and
    unchanged state when the effect is declined or its coin flip fails.

10.14G [LOCKED] C-RELEASE-GATE-014 — PRINTED STATUS TICK AMOUNTS (v2.19)
  * A printed stronger Poison (e.g., DP Skuntank Toxic Cloud, two damage
    counters) requires amount=20 in the status record; ordinary Poison is
    amount=10. Verify the between-turn tick with the proper phase and do
    not mistake automatic Sleep recovery or normal Weakness for an effect
    bug. Reversing the attack or target side must not alter the rule.
  * All self-damage, status, choice and Bench interaction smoke tests must
    include targeted, assertive outcome tests for any new shared handler.
    A validated effect object is only syntax, not proof of card correctness.

10.14H [LOCKED] C-RELEASE-GATE-015 — PER-POKEMON PAYABLE ATTACK / CPU ENERGY (v2.19)
  * A Pokemon may have a printed off-type attack but still be playable on a
    mono-Energy plan if at least one different attack is fully payable.
    DP Steelix/Onix is the canonical example: Onix's Colorless Headbutt
    works from Metal Energy and Steelix's Metal attacks work from Metal.
  * For DP1-only CPU builds, inspect every included Pokemon's attack costs,
    not a global union of every optional printed cost. Require a legal
    30-card deck, only permitted cards and Basic Energy, at least one
    payable attack per Pokemon, all expected evolution stages, and true
    matching of the selected type. Do not relax older-set Energy rules
    as an accidental side effect of this DP-specific repair.
  * Run seeded opponent builds for all eight Basic Energy types after each
    new release, especially Metal. A Colorless-only requested archetype
    needs a real Basic Energy type supply; it does not imply that a
    printed Colorless Basic Energy card exists.
  * Partner metadata must be checked for Baby Evolution STACK payoffs even
    when the partner is a Basic or Stage 1 rather than a final Stage 2:
    DP Electabuzz's Shock Wave needs Elekid, and Clefairy's Moon Impact
    needs Cleffa. Choose no off-filter Energy solely for support Babies.
  * All LV.X remain gated until their Active-only Level-Up legality,
    previous-level inherited attacks/Powers, interaction/retreat/KO/Prize
    timing and player/CPU choices are implemented and verified.

10.14I [LOCKED] C-RELEASE-GATE-016 — ABILITY LOCATION AND TWO-SIDED DECISIONS (v2.20)
  * Before enabling activated abilities, verify exactly which location the
    source must occupy (Active, Bench, either) and gate the action accordingly.
    DP Unown A/B/C/D Powers are Bench-only. Test the legal Bench activation,
    the illegal Active attempt, the once-per-turn limit, and both CPU sides.
  * A printed 'each player may' clause needs TWO independent, side-owned
    decisions; never infer that the opponent agrees because the attacker did.
    Present both accept and decline, preserve player-private information,
    and test the choices on either side of the battle.
  * If an optional effect moves the Active Pokémon out of play (including
    bouncing an Unown), the game must enter promotion if a Bench is present;
    do not leave a permanent empty Active slot or silently pick a substitute.
  * Coin-flip Heads and Tails are distinct branches. A triggered search must
    only happen on Heads, with a legal Bench slot and an eligible card in the
    requested location. Do not substitute an unrelated Pokémon or off-set
    evolved card when none qualifies.

10.14J [LOCKED] C-RELEASE-GATE-017 — PHYSICAL DESTINATION AUDIT (v2.20)
  * Write explicit source and destination for every card in an effect: hand,
    deck, Bench, Prize, attached stack, ordinary discard and ordered Basic
    Energy ledger are not interchangeable. Verify physical UID/card identity
    and quantity before and after each transfer, including return-all cases.
  * 'Up to N' retrieval must allow early Done, handle an exhausted source,
    reject ineligible items and shuffle at the printed checkpoint even if
    the player stops early. DP Drifblim Wind Wave is the regression example.
  * When an attack returns an entire Pokémon and attached cards to the deck,
    include EVERY evolution, attached Tool, Special Energy and Basic Energy.
    For LuckyDex's separate Basic Energy ledger, reconstruct physical Basic
    cards using the CURRENT set's print/art when the printed destination is
    a deck; a discard-ledger transfer would be incorrect. DP Drifloon Blowing
    Wind is the reference regression, including a second-choice opponent.
  * Separately test self-damage, placed damage counters and attack damage:
    counters do not take printed Weakness or Resistance; direct attack damage
    does. Test different Active/Bench targets and choice of target owner.
  * Printed multi-step attackers must preserve the order of selecting targets,
    damage, action costs, card movement, KO, Prize and optional switching.
    A 'no crash' smoke result is never sufficient to un-gate a card.

10.14K [LOCKED] C-RELEASE-GATE-018 — ABILITY / DECKBUILDING / STAGE CLAIMS (v2.20)
  * Continuous Bodies on Benched Pokémon must interact with the actual allied
    Active and respect explicit Body suppression (Magnezone Magnetize). Verify
    the specific payable Energy and actual retreat cost, not just a displayed
    ability description.
  * Test Baby Evolution as an optional activated Power from HAND, with the
    right named partner, healed damage, preserved stack and selected deck
    synergy (Munchlax/Snorlax). An external-era partner must not be invented
    inside a set-only pool just because the Baby has a named target.
  * Every stage release must report exactly how many prints are 1) in the
    catalogue, 2) battle-enabled, and 3) still gated, and list each gated
    identity. At least one actual attack outcome, Power accept/decline,
    player+CPU path, focused/seeded deck and prior-era regression per shared
    mechanic is required before declaring it verified.
  * Do not relabel a staged set 'complete' until 100% of numbered prints and
    all new rule families (especially Pokémon LV.X Active-only level-up,
    previous-level attack/Power inheritance, KO and Prize behaviour) have
    dedicated tests and realistic long-battle/on-device follow-up.

10.15 [CHECK] C-DP1-ERA-001 — PRINTED ERA ISOLATION
  D&P 2007 uses printed ADDITIVE Weakness (+10/+20/+30) and reduction Resistance
  (-20). Apply the printed amount only in applicable era/print; never globally
  replace legacy ×2 calculations. DP Basic Darkness/Metal are true Basic Energy
  (not interchangeable with historical Special Energy prints). A Pokémon LV.X
  levels up ONLY from its named ACTIVE previous-level card and inherits that
  level's attacks/Powers/Bodies; it is not a standalone Basic/Stage 1/Stage 2.
  D&P opening-turn draw/Trainer-Stadium-Supporter restrictions must be scoped to
  applicable games, not backported to legacy EX battles; verify user-approved
  30-card Classic adaptation, implement and test before advertising full DP1.
  Current v0.69.222 STAGE is NOT complete: LV.X, changed opening rules and
  most abilities remain battle-gated. This era-specific rule is a REQUIRED
  future release gate, not proof that all its mechanics have shipped.

  [CURRENT] v0.69.185 targeted tests for recent EX Deoxys changes were reported as 67 passing; the wider historical run reported 370 passing, 5 obsolete version/UI assertion failures. These are HISTORICAL test outcomes, not guarantees about any later build.
  [CURRENT] v0.69.186 restores EX Deoxys Ninjask Slash’s missing runtime damage rule and fixes next-turn bonus display for older sets. C-ATTACK-001 regression tests cover actual damage, visible previews, expiry, both players, and Weakness applied after base-power replacement.
  [CHECK] Recheck source availability and compatibility before claiming any given card/expansion is 'fully correct'; the existence of a card record does not prove every unusual interaction.
  [CURRENT] Diamond & Pearl (dp1) v0.69.227 STAGE 6 — 130 original prints (107 Pokémon, 15 Trainers, 8 Basic Energy), 110/130 battle-enabled (91 Pokémon, 11 Trainers, 8 Basic Energy); 20 gated including 3 LV.X.   [CURRENT] Tests: v07187-ex-emerald.test.cjs and v07187-ex-emerald-audit.test.cjs exercise all attacks through two random seeds, legal CPU/player deck construction, Form Change, Special Energy, discards, damage counters and conditional Powers/Bodies. Node tests do not replace touch-device QA.

  [CURRENT] v0.69.190 adds EX Unseen Forces (ex10): 145 registered card identities (117 numbered and 28 letter/symbol Unown cards). Printed attacks, Powers/Bodies, Energy and Trainer text are represented in the catalogue, the Classic set picker and the player/CPU deck-building filters. A smaller reviewed subset is battleEnabled and deckEligible; all other cards are explicitly PARTIAL and cannot enter legal battle decks. Do not claim 145 fully battle-ready cards. See tests/v07190-classic-unseen-forces.test.cjs.
  [CURRENT] EX10 Unown share one printed Pokémon NAME for copy-limit checks, but each printed letter/symbol is a distinct collection/card ID. The Shuffle Poké-Power has a single shared once-per-turn use and must preserve attached cards, damage and status across form changes. EX10 Pokémon ★ have a printed one-per-deck restriction, which needs engine-level legality enforcement before they become battleEnabled.
  [LOCKED] C-AI-ENERGY-001: Classic CPU must evaluate and use BOTH independent normal attachments: the current Energy Zone unit and one physical Basic/Special Energy card from hand. A CPU may not spend turns accumulating recovered/searched Energy in its hand without considering a legal hand attachment before attacking or ending its turn. Respect individual card restrictions, board targets and the existing attachment limits.
  [LOCKED] C-AI-CHOICE-002: The CPU must not habitually select 'Skip' for a beneficial optional effect after it has already played the card or used the attack. This includes Energy Search, Energy Recycle System, Unown Q search, healing, targeted acceleration and discarded Energy retrieval. An optional cost or strategically disadvantageous effect may still be declined after evaluation.
  [LOCKED] C-AI-HEAL-003: Do not play a healing Trainer when all eligible Pokémon are undamaged; when healing is legal, the selected Pokémon must actually receive the effect. At minimum, evaluate current damage and target choice rather than play-and-skip.
  [BUG/FIXED] EX Unseen Forces Energy Recycle System's internal `mode=hand/deck` was previously rejected by the generic attack-damage-mode validator when resolving its second choice. Its internal selection field is now `recoveryMode`. This is a battle-engine exception that could force a manual CPU Resume; verify both 1-to-hand and 3-to-deck results.
  [CURRENT] v0.69.191 reviewed all 89 executable attacks on the 65 battle-enabled EX Unseen Forces cards through a deterministic smoke sweep, audited and corrected the EX10 support Trainer metadata for Energy Recycle System, Energy Search, Mary’s Request and Potion, and added targeted CPU turn/choice regression coverage. This is not an assertion that all 145 cards are playable, that every optional branch was exercised, or that Android touchscreen execution was tested.
  [CURRENT] v0.69.190 old Base/Jungle/Fossil fixes: generic any-card searches no longer offer the Basic Energy Zone (e.g. Computer Search); focused Basic/Stage-1 strategies such as Scyther work even with later unrelated evolutions; sparse legal Fossil focus builds may use a compatible secondary type when the user did not lock to a single type. Neo Discovery Metal Scizor can generate Metal + Grass for its Scyther line, and Darkness Tyranitar can generate Darkness + Fighting for its Pupitar line, even if the player selected only the final Pokémon type; these no longer require owned Neo Genesis Special Energy. Explicit Energy-specific search still uses the agreed Zone adapter.
  [CURRENT] v0.69.192 enables a further 22 EX Unseen Forces (ex10) cards after implementing missing attack/Body/Trainer paths. Reviewed playable subset: 87 cards with 123 attacks, 58 cards still PARTIAL/battle-disabled. Specific new coverage includes Jolteon, Vaporeon, Octillery Super Suction Cups, Slowbro Dual Armor, Granbull, Magcargo, Clefable, Scyther, Gligar, Slowpoke, Energy Removal 2, Energy Switch, Poké Ball, Professor Elm's Training Method, Politoed ex, Scizor ex, the three Pokémon ★, and Unown D/O. A printed-card regression verifies fixed card identities, damage, energy zones, choice owners, and legal CPU decks. Do NOT silently promote the remaining 58 cards (notably numerous evolution-triggered Powers, multi-step Tools, and complex Unown Hidden Powers) without real outcome tests.
  [LOCKED / EXISTING C-CHOICE] EX10 targeting: Energy Removal 2 and Energy Switch choose the actual Pokémon by tapping board slots; the player then selects the specific Energy (Basic or Special where eligible). Unown D chooses the affected side then the exact evolved Pokémon. Granbull Crushing Blow chooses the actual attached Energy, not the first Energy in memory. Required switches are not optional once a legal Bench target exists. This is an application of the existing direct-targeting rule, not permission to choose for an opponent where printed text says the opponent chooses.
  [LOCKED / EXISTING C-ENERGY] EX10 Vaporeon Aqua Supply attaches a Basic Energy actually held in hand; it does not conjure a free Zone unit. Slowpoke Fishing Tail recovers actual discard cards or the Basic Energy discard. Energy Switch transfers, without duplicating, an attached Basic Energy. Energy Removal 2 sends chosen Basic Energy to its original owner's Basic Energy discard and Special Energy to their physical discard.
  [CURRENT] v0.69.192 tested the larger playable subset in deterministic EX10 attack execution sweeps, 19 new card-outcome tests, the existing EX10 CPU and card-identity tests, 251 EX Hidden Legends-through-EX Emerald checks, and 44 selected older Base/Jungle/Fossil checks. A large all-at-once historic runner timed out; smaller suites completed. These are Node checks, not Android touch or an exhaustive proof of all combinations. See docs/TCG_EX_UNSEEN_FORCES_BATCH_v0.69.192.md.
  [CURRENT] v0.69.193 corrects the EX10 single-set Quilava/Cyndaquil screenshot regression: EX10 Typhlosion is PARTIAL, so a random EX10-only builder must not strand Quilava; an owned, battle-ready Typhlosion from Neo Genesis can complete the focused line when that set is selected. Unrelated Scyther filler is excluded. The shared builder, saved-deck Energy analysis, themed metadata and CPU opponent generation now recognise Dual Armor's conditional activation Energy. Tests/v07193-classic-evolution-dual-armor.test.cjs covers the original screenshot seed, positive/negative evolution eligibility, dual types, metadata and CPU; the original v07191 late-game CPU fixture is pinned to the original 30-card decks to preserve its >75-action regression across future deck-builder changes.
  [CURRENT] v0.69.194 completes the implementation pass for the remaining EX Unseen Forces subset: the registry now marks 145/145 cards battle-enabled, including 124 Pokémon with 196 printed attacks, 16 Trainers and 5 Special Energy. The previously gated 58 cards have named effect/ability handlers and the all-attacks execution sweep resolves the supported choice flow; this status is NOT a claim every matchup or Android UI path has been exercised. The 28 letter/symbol Unown have distinct attack semantics, same-name copy-limit rules and their shared Shuffle Power. Pokémon ex evolution triggers, Baby Evolution, Energy Evolution, type/attack/Power restrictions and cross-zone Energy remain subject to printed conditions.
  [LOCKED] C-EX10-TRIGGER-001: EX10 triggered Poké-Powers activate only at their printed time (evolution from hand, Energy attached from HAND, damage/KO event) and must have valid source/target ownership. The trigger must not be selectable as an unrestricted once-per-turn button. Extra Energy sourced by a printed DECK search uses the approved Classic Zone conversion; a HAND-only ability requires a real card in hand.
  [LOCKED] C-EX10-TOOLS-002: EX10 Energy Root, Sitrus Berry, Fluffy Berry, Solid Rage, Curse Powder and Protective Orb respect eligibility, timing, return/discard, Bench/Active and exclusion of Pokémon-ex as printed. A Tool's HP bonus does not vanish when its own effect suppresses the attached Pokémon's Poké-Power/Body.
  [LOCKED] C-EX10-TOOLS-003: Energy Root ex10-83 is ATTACHABLE to an
    otherwise legal Pokémon even if it currently has Poké-Powers or
    Poké-Bodies; it suppresses those abilities but gives +20 MAX HP while
    worn. It cannot stay on Pokémon-ex, Dark-named or owner-named Pokémon.
    Correctly validate the card in the TOOL targeting path, not the Energy
    attachment path. Fluffy Berry ex10-85 and Solid Rage ex10-92 are also
    real persistent Tools and must have a valid while-attached duration.
  [LOCKED] C-DECK-TOOL-001: An automatic player/CPU deck must not add Energy
    Root when every otherwise eligible Pokémon relies on a Power or Body;
    preserving those abilities is better than adding a dead/sabotaging Tool.
    Manual play may still intentionally use Energy Root to suppress a Power.
  [LOCKED] C-EX10-DECK-003: Auto-builder must include a playable higher evolution in the featured line even after an expansion gains newly FULL cards. A Quilava focus in ex10 now includes ex10 Typhlosion as well as Cyndaquil and Quilava; this supersedes the v193 assumption that the ex10 Typhlosion was still PARTIAL. Genuine dual-type attack/conditional Energy plans remain preserved for both player and CPU.
  [CURRENT] Node regression evidence for v0.69.194: 78/78 EX10/CPU/builder tests, 112/112 EX Deoxys, EX Emerald and hybrid Energy-routing checks, and 34/34 selected older Base/Jungle/Fossil checks. These are NOT hardware/phone touch tests and cannot establish every unusual attack branch. The 145-card status requires continued bug follow-up if live gameplay reveals mismatches.
  [CURRENT] v0.69.196 registers 114 authentic EX Delta Species (ex11) card prints: 95 Pokémon, 14 Trainers, 5 Special Energy. 82 are implementation-gated as battle-ready; 32 are explicitly PARTIAL and excluded from player/CPU legal battle decks. This is intentionally a staged rollout, NOT a 114-card implementation claim. See docs/TCG_EX_DELTA_SPECIES_069196_RELEASE_NOTES.md.
  [LOCKED] C-EX11-DELTA-001: δ Pokémon retain every printed type for Weakness, Resistance, attack damage and Energy requirements. Evolution matching strips the δ mark only for species compatibility; card identity, artist print and set number remain distinct. Auto player/CPU decks must include playable evolution stages and all required Energy types rather than force a single printed type.
  [LOCKED] C-EX11-HOLON-002: Holon Energy FF / GL / WP provide Colorless Energy. Their printed conditional effects require the corresponding physically attached Basic Energy (Fire/Fighting, Grass/Lightning, Water/Psychic), and are ignored on Pokémon-ex. Special Metal/Darkness retain the proper EX-era print-specific rules.
  [LOCKED] C-EX11-HOLON-003: Holon's Magnemite, Voltorb, Magneton and Electrode may be attached as Special Energy under their printed rules, but may NOT enter legal battles until that alternate card-play and Energy-return path works for both player and CPU. Do not re-label these cards FULL just because their Pokémon attacks work.
  [LOCKED] C-EX11-ENERGY-004: A card effect recovering discarded Basic Energy uses the separate Basic Energy discard AND eligible physical Basic Energy in the normal discard. Recovering a physical Basic Energy must attach it as Basic, never incorrectly as Special. A printed deck search specifically for fresh Basic Energy can use the ordered Energy Zone, including EX11 Starmie δ Metal Navigation; an eligible physical card in the draw deck remains an additional selectable source. Hand-only effects still require a real hand card.
  [LOCKED] C-EX11-TRAINER-005: Holon Supporters with a mandatory discard cost cannot be used without another discardable hand card. They must visibly request the discarded card, and no draw/search may create duplicate Energy or exceed the Classic 8-card hand cap.
  [CURRENT] The 32 PARTIAL cards include specialist Body/Power and event rules (for example Metagross δ, Tyranitar δ, Hypno, Slowking), four Holon Pokémon-as-Energy prints, complex Stadium/Supporter effects and three Pokémon ★. Future completion passes must add exact printed outcomes, choice ownership, alternate-Energy semantics, AI and live-battle regressions before unlocking each card.
  [LOCKED] C-EX10-UNOWN-020: EX Unseen Forces Unown W (ex10-W) Hidden Power has a printed base of 20 damage. Its optional coin-dependent card recovery to the top of the draw deck is in addition to damage, not a replacement for damage. Both battle damage and UI preview must show the correct base where no modifiers apply; card recovery remains source/destination-correct.
  [LOCKED] C-EX11-BODY-006: EX Delta Species Shelgon δ (ex11-54) Exoskeleton reduces incoming attack damage by 10 after Weakness/Resistance. Secret Azumarill (ex11-114) Thick Fat reduces attack damage from Fire/Water Pokémon by 30 after Weakness/Resistance, and does not prevent damage from unrelated types. Both depend on the Body being active (Tools, suppression and Stadiums may disable it).
  [LOCKED] C-EX11-CHOICE-007: EX Delta Species Vaporeon ex (ex11-110) Evolutionary Swirl triggers on evolution from hand; the owner may decline or shuffle the opponent's hand into the draw deck. If shuffled, the OPPONENT chooses how many cards to draw, from zero to four, subject to its available deck/hand cap. Never force four cards or let the attacker choose the defender's count.
  [LOCKED] C-EX11-META-008: EX Delta Species search Powers count as search, not blind draw; moving existing Energy between Pokémon is Energy transfer, not free Energy acceleration; drawing for the opponent through disruption is not self-draw support. Holon Energy FF/GL/WP record the Basic Energy types that enable their conditional effects, without pretending they themselves supply those Basic types.
  [CURRENT] v0.69.197 second audit leaves EX Delta Species at 82/114 battle-ready, 32 explicitly gated PARTIAL. Added result-level regressions for Unown W/other fixed-20 Hidden Powers, Exoskeleton/Thick Fat, selected EX11 damage and attack restrictions, Vaporeon ex opponent choice, and deck metadata. Twelve seeded EX11 AI simulations cover all four difficulties. The broad EX-era + Energy regression is 209/209 passed; selected historical suites are 46/52, with the same six failing in v0.69.196. Node smoke and targeted checks do not prove every printed branch or Android touchscreen flow.
  NEXT PLANNED SET: Finish the remaining 32 EX Delta Species PARTIAL cards before EX Legend Maker (ex12), unless the user directs otherwise.

=======================================================================
PART 12 — OPEN QUESTIONS / DO NOT SILENTLY DECIDE
=======================================================================

  [CHECK-01] Classic Bench and victory format: historical approved design was five Bench spaces and four-point victory with prize values; inspected v0.69.185 engine uses three Bench spaces and five physical Prize cards. Requires user decision before an overall-format redesign.
  [CHECK-02] Decide what happens to unusual official rules involving an explicitly second Defending Pokémon, expanded Bench or physical prize placement in this converted format, card by card. Maintain existing documented adaptations until a specific better rule is agreed.
  [RESOLVED-03 / C-ENERGY-007] Previously open: any effect that explicitly shuffles eligible Basic Energy into the deck must put REAL DRAWABLE cards in the DRAW DECK. No generic diversion back to Energy Zone; see 3.2b. Check unique card-specific exceptions only when their printed text actually uses a different destination.
  [CHECK-04] Any historical Pocket or Classic bug list needs a current repro before it is claimed to remain open; do not infer unresolved bugs purely from old release notes.
  [CHECK-05] Large custom Stadium Bench limits and varied hand/discard/card-picker UX should be checked on real Android touch hardware even where Node assertions pass.
